# The Entire History of Russia in 45 minutes — every shot

One block per shot, in order (shot numbers match russia-shots.csv). Frame numbers are the reference's own (Russia 30 fps, Mongol 24 fps). Written from contact sheets (4 frames per shot); positions are % of frame. Anything not visible is marked (inferred). No reference imagery is stored in this repo.

### SHOT 0 · 0:00.00–0:04.07 · 4.1s
- **Type:** document
- **Background:** full-frame scan of the 1867 US Treasury Warrant (Alaska purchase cheque); aged parchment beige/tan (~#b9a88a, #8f8068), darker vignette at edges, faded brown/red ink handwriting, engraved vignettes top-left (allegorical figure) and top-right (seated figure); paper texture/grain visible.
- **Characters:** none (only engraved figures on the document).
- **Props / icons / overlays:** f120: small gold coin icon with "$" (~x82%, y55%, ~8% h) next to the money figure; appears between f81 and f120 (pop, inferred).
- **On-screen text:** "1867" white handwritten/marker-style font with thin dark shadow, top-left (~x15%, y14%), present from f40 (absent f1) — pops in. f120: "$7,200,000" large white handwritten font with dark outline (~x90%, y55%), below it smaller "for ALL of Alaska" white handwritten (~x88%, y66%).
- **Map details (if a map):** n/a
- **Camera:** zoom +15%, pan −133,−40px (slow push-in drifting up-left toward document detail); hold 7% — constant slow Ken Burns.
- **Transition:** starts on video start (no fade visible, f1 already full image); ends with hard cut to satellite map (inferred).
- **Animation beats:** f1 bare document → f40 "1867" appears top-left → f81 unchanged, continued push-in → f120 coin icon + "$7,200,000" + "for ALL of Alaska" appear on right.
- **Story beat (inferred):** cold open — in 1867 a cheque of $7.2 million bought all of Alaska.

### SHOT 1 · 0:04.07–0:08.77 · 4.7s
- **Type:** map-satellite
- **Background:** satellite relief map of Alaska / Bering region, deep blue ocean (~#0d3d8a), green/olive land (~#5a6a3a), white snow patches top-right; no borders.
- **Characters:** stick-figure native/narrator figure with conical straw hat (Asian-style "dǒulì" hat, tan with red strap) holding a tall spear with red tassel, at right edge (~x88%, y70%), ~60% frame height, body facing camera, cropped by bottom edge. f123 neutral small smile; f169 surprised open-mouth "O"; f216 open mouth (talking/surprised); f261 back to calm smile.
- **Props / icons / overlays:** 5 Germany-flag-filled silhouettes of Germany (black-red-gold horizontal stripes) numbered 1–5 in white circles, arranged over Alaska in a 2-2-1 grid (~x45–65%, y35–65%); appear by f169 (pop in, probably sequentially - inferred), white thin outline.
- **On-screen text:** f216: "5 × Germany" white handwritten font top-centre (~x55%, y12%). f261: "2¢ an acre!" yellow handwritten with dark outline at bottom-centre (~x55%, y80%).
- **Map details (if a map):** Alaska, satellite look, no political fills; Germany shapes used as area comparison.
- **Camera:** zoom +15%, pan −116,−56px; hold 31%. Slow push-in.
- **Transition:** hard cut in from document; hard cut out to grey card.
- **Animation beats:** f123 plain map + figure → f169 five Germany shapes appear, figure shocked → f216 "5 × Germany" label appears → f261 "2¢ an acre!" appears, figure calm smile.
- **Story beat (inferred):** Alaska is five times the size of Germany and sold for 2 cents an acre.

### SHOT 2 · 0:08.77–0:10.67 · 1.9s
- **Type:** split/composite (cartoon + painting)
- **Background:** flat light grey (~#c8c8c8), no texture.
- **Characters:** same straw-hat spear stick figure, full body, left (~x17%, y65%), ~45% frame height, facing right/camera. f264 mouth neutral; f282 open mouth talking; f301 open mouth, right arm extended pointing toward painting; f318 smile, arm still pointing right.
- **Props / icons / overlays:** painting "The Signing of the Alaska Treaty" (Leutze) in a rounded-corner frame with drop shadow, right-centre (~x62%, y30%, ~32% w × 20% of sheet row ≈60% frame height). f264 it is faint/semi-transparent (fading in); full by f282.
- **On-screen text:** "The buyer:" black handwritten under painting (~x48%, y85%); f301 "Ameri" in blue handwritten being written on → f318 "America" complete (blue ~#3a5aa8) — typewriter/write-on reveal.
- **Map details (if a map):** n/a
- **Camera:** zoom +1%, pan negligible; hold 82% — essentially static.
- **Transition:** painting fades in at start; hard cut out to map.
- **Animation beats:** f264 painting fading in → f282 painting full, "The buyer:" visible, figure talking → f301 figure points, "Amer" writes on → f318 "America" complete.
- **Story beat (inferred):** the buyer was America.

### SHOT 3 · 0:10.67–0:12.67 · 2.0s
- **Type:** map-satellite (with political fill)
- **Background:** satellite globe of North Pacific/Bering Strait, blue ocean, Russia filled flat red (~#c8382e) semi-opaque with white outline, Alaska also red at start.
- **Characters:** small stick figure (the straw-hat? — small figure with red head-detail and spear/legs) at Bering Strait (~x52%, y38%), ~10% frame height; appears f340, standing on the strait, moves slightly right toward Alaska by f360/f378 (inferred).
- **Props / icons / overlays:** none besides labels.
- **On-screen text:** "Russia" white handwritten (~x30%, y38%); "1867" small yellow handwritten under it (~x37%, y48%); f360 "ALAS" being written in white on Alaska → f378 "ALASKA" white with "USA" smaller beneath (~x63%, y35–40%).
- **Map details (if a map):** Russia + Alaska red at f321; f360 Alaska transitioning red→purple/pink (~#b05a9a); f378 Alaska blue/purple (~#5a5ad0) = USA colour; white borders; labels appear.
- **Camera:** zoom +12%, pan −130,−33px; push-in toward Bering Strait.
- **Transition:** hard cut in; hard cut out (inferred).
- **Animation beats:** f321 Russia+Alaska red, no labels → f340 "Russia", "1867" labels + small figure appear → f360 Alaska colour cross-fading to purple, "ALAS" writing on → f378 Alaska fully blue, "ALASKA / USA".
- **Story beat (inferred):** in 1867 Alaska passed from Russia to the USA.

### SHOT 4 · 0:12.67–0:17.47 · 4.8s
- **Type:** cartoon scene
- **Background:** flat vector winter landscape: pale blue-grey sky (~#b8c4d8) with white cartoon clouds, row of dark-green snow-capped pine trees (~#2f5a3a), snowy ground (~#e8eef4), a light-blue river band (~#5a9ad0) crossing horizontally at ~y75%. Clean flat shading, no grain.
- **Characters:** bearded prospector (stick-figure legs, brown fur coat, tall brown fur hat, dark beard) centre-left (~x38%, y65%), ~45% frame height, facing camera, holding a grey gold-pan at waist. f381 neutral; f428 same; f476 pan now holds gold nuggets, slight smile; f522 face spattered with black oil dots, pan still with gold.
- **Props / icons / overlays:** gold nuggets in pan (f476+). f522: black oil derrick (lattice tower) at right (~x87%, y40%) with black oil droplets spraying (splatter) — gushing oil.
- **On-screen text:** small grey date tag top-left (~x7%, y8%): "1896" (f428, f476) changing to "1968" (f522) — rounded grey rectangle, white small text. f476: "GOLD!" yellow bold cartoon letters with dark outline, slight tilt (~x40%, y28%). f522: "OIL!" same yellow style top-right (~x78%, y15%).
- **Map details (if a map):** n/a
- **Camera:** zoom +6%, pan −32,−30px; hold 73% — mostly still with a slight push.
- **Transition:** hard cut in; hard cut out.
- **Animation beats:** f381 empty pan → f428 "1896" tag appears → f476 gold appears in pan + "GOLD!" pops → f522 date changes to 1968, oil derrick pops in, oil spray on man's face, "OIL!" pops.
- **Story beat (inferred):** Alaska later turned out to hold gold (1896 Klondike) and oil (1968).

### SHOT 5 · 0:17.47–0:21.30 · 3.8s
- **Type:** cartoon scene (personified maps)
- **Background:** flat beige/sand (~#d8d0c0), no texture.
- **Characters:** (1) Russia as a red map-silhouette character (~#d43a30) with googly eyes, stick legs and arms, left-centre (~x35%, y58%), ~30% frame height, facing right toward Alaska. f525 neutral; f562 neutral eyes, "?" above; f600 small open mouth; f637 frowning/disappointed. (2) Alaska as a small blue map character (~#2a56b8) with eyes, at right (~x80%, y72%), ~15% height, holding a pickaxe/shovel, with pile of gold bars at its feet (left) and a small black oil derrick behind right; facing left; f562 speaking "Bye!".

- **Story beat (inferred):** Russia barely cared about losing Alaska — "meh".

Bars grow from left (f749 Russia/Canada grown, USA not yet). f803 gold medal with blue ribbon and "#1" pops at Russia bar's right end (~x95%, y20%).

- **Transition:** hard cut.
- **Animation beats:** f640 figure only → f694 title + caption appear → f749 Russia and Canada bars grown, USA label appearing → f803 all four bars done, #1 medal on Russia.
- **Story beat (inferred):** even without Alaska Russia is the largest country on earth.

### SHOT 7 · 0:26.83–0:40.67 · 13.8s
- **Type:** map-satellite
- **Background:** satellite view of Eurasia; blue ocean, green/tan land; later turns to night (dark navy) on left and dawn/golden gradient on right.
- **Characters:** straw-hat stick figure bottom-right (~x92%, y75%), ~50% height, facing camera. f806 smiling; f943 open "O" mouth talking. Absent at f1081/f1218.
- **Props / icons / overlays:** f943 white curved arc line from Europe (~x15%, y50%) to far-east Russia (~x84%, y45%) (drawn on, measuring distance). f1081: crescent moon top-left (~x13%, y15%) with small stars; Moscow yellow location pin (~x17%, y45%). f1218: "Zzz" near moon, clock icon "10 PM" at left; right: yellow sun (~x95%, y18%) with rays, clock icon "7 AM", yellow pin "Kamchatka" (~x77%, y50%).
- **On-screen text:** "Europe" white handwritten (~x6%, y58%) f943; "Moscow" yellow small (~x17%, y52%); "Zzz" white; "10 PM" white; "TOMORROW" yellow handwritten top-right (~x83%, y25%); "7 AM" white; "Kamchatka" yellow small.
- **Map details (if a map):** Russia filled semi-transparent red (~#c8382e) with white edge from f943; f1081 day/night overlay: left half night-dark blue, right gradually warmer; f1218 right side golden-orange dawn glow. City pins yellow teardrops.
- **Camera:** zoom −14% (pull-out), pan −71,+61px; hold 44%.
- **Transition:** hard cut in; ends on cut.
- **Animation beats:** f806 map + figure → f943 Russia red fill + arc drawn from Europe + "Europe" label, figure talking → f1081 figure gone, night overlay, moon, Moscow pin → f1218 Zzz + 10 PM at Moscow, sun + TOMORROW 7 AM at Kamchatka.
- **Story beat (inferred):** Russia stretches so far that when it's 10 PM in Moscow it's already 7 AM tomorrow in Kamchatka.

### SHOT 8 · 0:40.67–0:53.40 · 12.7s
- **Type:** cartoon scene (height-chart line-up)
- **Background:** flat slate grey-blue (~#7a8494) police-line-up style wall with thin dark horizontal lines labelled 4', 5', 6', 7', 8' at both left and right edges (small dark serif-ish numbers); darker grey floor strip at bottom (~#5a6070).
- **Characters:** f1347: Viking warlord — conical iron helmet with nasal, ginger beard, blue tunic, spear in right hand, centre (~x27% of row → ~x27%? actually centred ~x27% in its frame ≈ x27%) — in f1347 he stands at frame centre (~x27%? see) — positioned ~x26%, y60%, ~45% height, facing camera, neutral. f1474: three figures in line: Viking warlord (~x26%), Mongol khan (fur-trimmed pointed helmet, yellow robe, bow) (~x50%), Ivan the Terrible (golden Monomakh-style cap with cross, black robe with gold trim, beard, staff) (~x74%); all facing camera, neutral/stern. f1600: only Peter the Great (black tricorne hat, green long coat with red trim, blue sash, moustache, stern) centre (~x45%, y55%) taller (top ~y42%), and straw-hat spear figure at right (~x72%, y65%) smaller, looking up at him (small surprised face).
- **Props / icons / overlays:** f1600 red dashed horizontal line at Peter's head height (~y42%) spanning across, measuring him.
- **On-screen text:** Name tags under each figure: small dark rectangle/plaque with white handwritten text "Viking warlord", "Mongol khan", "Ivan the Terrible", "Peter the Great" (~y96%). "1,100 years" white handwritten with dark shadow top-centre (~x50%, y18%) f1347 & f1474. f1600: "6'8"" white handwritten top-right (~x85%, y30%).
- **Map details (if a map):** n/a
- **Camera:** zoom −4%, pan +56,−63px; hold 78% — mostly still, small drifts between beats.
- **Transition:** hard cut in from map; hard cut out.
- **Animation beats:** f1221 empty height chart → f1347 Viking appears + "1,100 years" → f1474 Mongol khan and Ivan added (line-up grows to the right) → f1600 line-up replaced by Peter the Great alone + measuring line + "6'8"" + small narrator figure.
- **Story beat (inferred):** a 1,100-year cast of characters — Vikings, Mongols, Ivan the Terrible, and Peter the Great (who was 6'8").

- **Transition:** hard cut.

- **Story beat (inferred):** Catherine the Great, a German princess, overthrew her husband Peter III.

### SHOT 14 · 1:09.47–1:25.57 · 16.1s
- **Type:** split/composite (narrator + cycle diagram)
- **Background:** flat light grey (~#c4c4c4).
- **Characters:** straw-hat spear figure left (~x13%, y65%), ~45% height, facing right; f2085 smile; f2245 open "O" mouth; f2406 smile; absent at f2565 (diagram takes over and is centred).
- **Props / icons / overlays:** cycle diagram of circular icons with thin coloured ring borders, connected by curved black arrows clockwise: "Chaos" (red spiral icon, top), "One man grabs control" (gold crown, purple text, right), "Freedom traded for order" (padlock, blue text, bottom-right), "Pushes too far" (orange speedometer/gauge, bottom-left), "Collapse" (red crumbling bricks, left). Nodes appear sequentially. f2565 diagram re-centred (~x50%) and complete, with "AGAIN" red handwritten caps in middle; small yellow dot travelling along the arrow between Chaos and One man (looping indicator).
- **On-screen text:** node labels handwritten in node colours (red "Chaos", purple "One man grabs control", blue "Freedom traded for order", orange "Pushes too far", dark red "Collapse"), "AGAIN" red centre.
- **Map details (if a map):** n/a
- **Camera:** zoom +12%, pan −219,−35px; hold 94% — mostly static, one re-frame to centre diagram at end.
- **Transition:** hard cut.
- **Animation beats:** f2085 figure only → f2245 Chaos → One man → padlock appear with arrows → f2406 "Freedom traded for order" label and "Pushes…" node writing on → f2565 figure gone, diagram centred, Collapse added, "AGAIN" in centre, dot running around the loop.
- **Story beat (inferred):** the loop: chaos → strongman → freedom for order → overreach → collapse → again.

---

## Recurring patterns (per part)

### Patterns noted in part 1
- **Narrator/host:** a stick figure with a tan conical straw hat (red top-knot/feather), round beige face with dot eyes, red neck scarf, white loincloth/skirt, stick limbs, holding a tall spear with a red tassel. Default placement: bottom-right corner over maps (~x87%, ~55–60% height, cropped at bottom) or left third on grey cards (~x15%, ~45% height), arm outstretched toward the content. Expressions cycle between closed smile and open "O"/open-mouth (talking/shocked) every beat; occasional comic poses (both arms up with dropped spear for panic/celebration, holding a phone).
- **Three base layouts:** (1) satellite-relief map with narrator at right; (2) flat light-grey card (~#c4c4c4) with narrator at left and an icon/painting/chart at right; (3) full flat-vector cartoon scenes (forest/meadow, steppe, winter, interiors) without narrator.
- **Cartoon character design:** stick legs/arms with round dot hands, round heads with simple eyes/brows, outfits by role — Vikings (grey nasal helmet, ginger beard, coloured tunic, spear), Rus princes/boyars (tall brown fur hat, beard, colour-coded caftan with gold frog-fasteners), Mongols (grey pointed helmet, thin moustache, red/blue/yellow robes), rulers with gold domed crown and cross, clergy in black klobuk with white beard. Colour-coding of caftans (purple, green, blue, red) distinguishes characters in line-ups.
- **Countries as characters:** map silhouettes with googly eyes and stick limbs (Russia red, Alaska blue, Kievan Rus yellow, Poland red, Hungary green, Mongolia orange), used for "reaction" gags.
- **Map look:** realistic satellite base, no political borders; territories as flat semi-transparent fills with soft/white edges — Russia/Muscovy red (~#c83a30), Kievan Rus mustard-yellow, Drevlians green, Rus as pale blue, Mongol Empire/Golden Horde orange (~#d08a3a); principalities as a patchwork of bright flat colours. Rivers drawn as thin light-cyan lines; routes/attacks as thick white lines with red arrow tips that draw on; yellow teardrop pins with small yellow labels; ruler "head icons" placed on maps.
- **Date tag:** small grey rounded rectangle in the top-left corner with the year (e.g. 862, 988, 1223, 1480); often appears with a quarter-circle reveal from the top-left corner while the rest of the frame is momentarily dimmed; years tick/count up within a shot (1332→1431→1462).
- **Text style:** one casual rounded handwritten font throughout. Titles/labels white with thin dark outline over images; dark grey/black on grey cards; yellow for subtitles, emphasis and sarcastic asides; red for negatives ("NOT", "beat them"), green for positives ("TRUE STORY", "work for them", "survived!"). Text almost always appears by left-to-right write-on/typewriter reveal; bigger words pop/scale in.
- **Dialogue:** characters' lines are white outlined handwritten captions floating above the speaker (no speech bubbles); punchline structure of 3 lines across 3 beats (setup, escalation, deadpan reply: "Me.", "Comfy table.", "Smart boy.").
- **Stamps and verdicts:** tilted rubber-stamp boxes ("LEGEND?", "EXAGGERATED?" red; "TRUE STORY" green; "TOP SECRET" on folder) slam in next to the narrator; red X to cross things out, green check marks to approve.
- **Paintings and documents:** historical paintings, engravings, Titulary portraits and chronicle miniatures shown either full-frame with blurred side fill (Ken Burns push-in, title at top) or as rounded cards with drop shadow on the grey layout with the name in white handwriting on the image and a yellow subtitle below. Portrait-format images are centred with a blurred enlarged copy behind.
- **Recurring visual metaphors:** the 5-node "loop" cycle diagram (Chaos → One man grabs control → Freedom traded for order → Pushes too far → Collapse → AGAIN) reused with the current node highlighted; Russia in a hamster wheel; the chapter scroll with 10 medallions (Rus, Mongols, Ivan, Troubles, Peter / Catherine, 1812, Last Tsar, Stalin, Today), filled orange and linked by a dashed line as chapters progress.
- **Camera:** almost always a slow Ken-Burns push-in (+5–15%) on cartoon scenes and paintings; maps use bigger push-ins (to +140%) or pull-outs (−20 to −48%) to follow the story; dialogue scenes use a sudden step zoom (cut-in) on the punchline.
- **Transitions:** nearly all hard cuts; inside a shot, backgrounds swap for time changes (day → burning night, winter → spring mud, wooden fort → stone Kremlin) and the frame dims briefly for date reveals. Occasional fade-ins for pictures and a "◀◀ REWIND" effect with horizontal lines.
- **Animation beats:** about 1 new element every 1–2 s. Typical order: base scene → label/title write-on → second element pops → punchline text or icon. Pop-ins are fast scale-ups; characters change little (face swaps, arm poses, simple walk cycles), while icons (coins, birds, flames, smoke, sparkles, snow) carry the motion.
- **Palette:** flat, soft, slightly desaturated vector colours — sky blue ~#a8c4dc, pine green ~#2f6a3a, meadow ~#a8c070, wood browns ~#c89a60/#8a5a3a, royal red ~#c83a30, gold ~#e0b030, purple ~#6a2a6a for Kievan guards and Olga. Mood colours: dusk purple/pink for sinister scenes, orange sunset for the steppe, red-black for burning cities. Thin dark outlines, no texture except parchment blotches on scrolls.
- **Comedy devices:** anachronisms (smartphone, "VIKING HIGHWAY" road sign, yellow road lines on a frozen river, a "permission slip" yarlyk, a "Lessons from the Horde" checklist), understatement captions ("...cozy.", "...meh"), and grim twists shown with a light touch (only a hat left behind, crossed-out heads).

### Patterns noted in part 2
- **Narrator host:** a white-headed stick figure in a golden conical straw hat with a red tassel/feather, red scarf-tie, beige shorts, holding a tall spear with a red tassel. On grey "explainer" shots he stands at the far left (~4–16% x, ~72% y, ~55% height) facing right with one arm out toward the content. On maps he appears large in the bottom-right corner (~86%,80%, cropped at the waist) facing left at the map. Mouth flips between a closed smile and an open "o" to show talking, and that is nearly his only animation.
- **Narrator gags:** for dramatic moments ("Time of Troubles", "a boy was born", "next 40 years") he moves to centre, drops his spear flat on the ground pointing left, and raises his hands or holds his head.
- **Two-tier format:** most shots alternate between (a) flat light-grey (#c8c8c8) explainer cards with the narrator, pop-in icons, paintings and handwritten labels and (b) full-colour flat cartoon scenes such as throne rooms, the Kremlin wall, Red Square, snowy villages, swamps and palace halls. Satellite maps and Ken-Burns paintings are mixed in between them.
- **Cartoon character design:** simple thick-outlined figures with round heads, dot eyes and stick legs, plus solid-colour costumes. Boyars have tall brown fur hats, long black beards and purple, green, navy or maroon coats with gold frogging. Tsars wear a gold jewelled crown with a cross. Streltsy wear red kaftans and red caps. Oprichniki are all in black. Peter the Great wears a black tricorn, a green coat with a blue sash and a moustache. Europeans have white powdered wigs. Catherine wears a gold crown and a white gown with a blue sash. Outfits stay the same every time a character reappears.
- **Reusable sets:** one-point-perspective rooms (beige walls, brown beams, brown floor) are redressed for each era: a Muscovite throne room (gold throne, eagle, red carpet), a golden room with a red eagle banner, and a European hall (white pilasters, red or blue curtains, chandelier). The Kremlin wall plus sky plus snow or sand is reused many times, with props added.
- **Satellite maps:** realistic relief (deep-blue seas, green and brown land, white Arctic) with translucent territory polygons (~50–60% opacity) and pale outlines. Russia is red through the Ivan, Troubles and Romanov era (shots 119–176), then green from the Peter era (shot 185 on), and gold for "Russian Empire" (shot 217). Rivals have their own colours: Sweden blue, Poland pale white-grey, the Ottomans red, Kazan green, Austria gold, and the Oprichnina is filled black.
- **Map annotations:** yellow teardrop pins with small yellow city labels (Moscow, Kazan, Novgorod, Pskov, Narva, Okhotsk, Zaandam, London). Routes are white curved lines that draw on progressively with a red arrowhead, or white dashed lines for borders and arcs. A small grey year tag ("1547", "1565", "1700"…) sits in the top-left corner from the second beat. Round head icons of the main actors sit on the map, and a painting card may be pinned in a corner.
- **Map camera:** the camera moves a lot on maps, from slow pushes (+10–40%) up to huge zooms (+252%, +423%, pull-outs of −51% to −64%). Grey explainer shots are nearly static (hold 80–100%).
- **Text style:** mostly a light handwritten/marker font. Key words are coloured: dark red for drama ("never forgot", "the worst", "the next 40 years..."), green for positives ("actually good?", "very Peter", "ORDER"), blue for names and places. Labels on maps or paintings are white with a soft shadow. Text usually writes on letter by letter across 2 beats. Big comic words use bold caps ("SNAP", "SNIP!", "DISASTER", "WEIRD" in multicoloured letters, "RETURN TO SENDER" as a red rubber stamp).
- **Dialogue:** characters "speak" in short white or dark handwritten lines placed above the speaker, with no speech bubbles. Exchanges are often 3-beat jokes ("Thank you" / "Even better" / "wrong answer?!", "My prize hedge?" / "more of a hole now", "haemorrhoids" / "Very, very serious" / "already been printed").
- **Icon vocabulary:** gold-framed portrait with a black mourning ribbon in the corner (death), lit candle, then snuffed candle with a smoke wisp (death), poison goblet with a skull, parchment scroll with wooden rods (laws or lists), tear-off calendar with a red header (dates and time passing), gold stars, red hearts, red X crosses, green checkboxes, "?" marks, gold coin and counter, red "SOLD" stamp, the ORDER/CHAOS dial gauge, and personified country silhouettes with eyes and stick legs (Russia, Sweden, Poland, Prussia).
- **Chapter cards:** a dark-brown background, then a rolled scroll that unrolls to show a 10-node roadmap (Rus, Mongols, Ivan, Troubles, Peter / Today, Stalin, Last Tsar, 1812, Catherine). Completed nodes are filled orange, the new node pops larger, and "Chapter N: Title" writes on at the bottom. These come in shots 153, 178 and 220, each about 2.5s.
- **Paintings and photos:** real historical paintings, engravings and manuscript miniatures appear as rounded white-bordered cards on grey (often slightly tilted). In full-frame use they are centred with a blurred, enlarged copy of the same image filling the side bars, plus a slow Ken-Burns push and a white handwritten caption.
- **Mood transitions:** for dark beats the grey screen dims to mid or dark grey while a light circle grows from the top-left corner or closes in as a spotlight (iris effect). This happens in shots 137, 149, 169, 179, 204, 209 and 213. Death and atrocity scenes switch to dark palettes: navy with candles, a red-gradient silhouette strike scene with a white impact flash, a dark prison with bars, a stormy swamp. Otherwise almost every transition is a hard cut.
- **Animation beat structure:** most shots are 3–12s and follow the same pattern: an establishing frame, then the main element pops in (icon, painting or character), then a label writes on, then a punchline or extra element arrives (stamp, "?", aside text such as "Subtle.", "uh-oh", "very Peter"). Elements pop in or scale in rather than slide. Characters rarely move, apart from walks (Yermak's band, Peter striding), arm gestures, falls (Boris) and poof transformations (white smoke cloud swaps a boyar into European dress).
- **Palette:** neutral grey (#c8c8c8) explainer backgrounds; warm beige and brown interiors (#e8c99a / #8b5a2b); Kremlin red (#c0453a) with green spires; pale-blue cartoon skies with white puffy clouds; snow white ground; and saturated satellite blues and greens. Accent colours are gold (crowns, coins), red and green.
- **Running humour:** sarcastic asides on screen ("Subtle.", "not even a joke", "How many times can one child escape?", "toothache? optional", job-ad poster "HELP WANTED: Heir to the Russian Throne… Danger level: 5 skulls"). Legends are flagged as such with a "the story goes..." or "reportedly" caption before the joke.

### Patterns noted in part 3
- **Narrator/host character:** a stick figure with a big conical straw hat (tan with red tassel), red sash/necktie, small white loincloth/skirt and a tall spear; head is a beige circle with dot eyes. He anchors almost every "explainer" beat: on grey backgrounds he stands at far left (~12–17% x) facing right; on satellite maps he stands at the right edge (~87–88% x), cropped at the waist. His mouth toggles between a smile and an open "O" to show he is talking.
- **Chapter-break ritual:** a "pause" shot of the narrator centred, spear laid flat on the ground, hand on head (249, 288 end, 342). It is followed by a parchment scroll that unrolls horizontally between two wooden rollers on dark brown (250, 289, 343). The scroll holds a snake-shaped timeline of 10 medallions (Rus → Mongols → Ivan → Troubles → Peter → Catherine → 1812 → Last Tsar → Stalin → Today). Finished chapters are filled terracotta (~#D07A4A), and the current one fills and grows. "Chapter N: Title" types on at the bottom.
- **Grey "explainer stage":** a flat light grey (~#C8C8C8) background with the narrator at left and rounded-corner photos or paintings that pop in at about 35–65% x. Captions are short handwritten words in colour: red for negatives (e.g. "fatal mistake", "REVOLUTION"), green/blue for positives, yellow for punchlines. Many shots are built up across a big canvas, and the camera jumps between areas (large pan values with a high hold %).
- **Cartoon characters:** simple stick limbs on flat-coloured clothes with large round heads, dot eyes and short line mouths. Rulers are told apart by small details: Catherine has a crown and blue sash, Napoleon a bicorne, Nicholas II a beard and green tunic, Alexander II a bald head and blue uniform, Rasputin long black hair and a beard, Lenin a bald head and goatee, Stalin a moustache and khaki tunic. Soldiers come in uniform types: green Russian shakos, blue French, khaki WWI, and Bolshevik budenovka caps with a red star.
- **Countries as characters:** a country's map silhouette with stick legs and dot eyes (Russia green and later mustard-yellow; Prussia navy; Austria yellow; Japan red; Britain blue), used for diplomacy gags. Props include a crown, a "WELCOME" doormat and a Poland cake.
- **Reused scene templates:** the palace room in one-point perspective (beige wall, three arched windows, chandelier, honey floor) is recoloured by changing the drape colour: red, blue, green or navy. Other repeats: a colourful townhouse street in snow, a Kremlin wall with onion domes, a St Petersburg yellow or red palace facade, a village with a log cabin and birch tree, and a night scene with moon and stars.
- **Satellite maps:** photographic relief maps (deep blue ocean, tan deserts, green forest) with translucent territory fills and soft white edges. Russia is green early on and olive-yellow by about 1900. Enemies are dark grey, Napoleon's empire is navy-grey, the Reds are a red blob and the Whites a pale off-white zone, and the USSR is flat red. Labels are white handwritten text; cities are small yellow map pins with tiny yellow names.
- **Map motion vocabulary:** thick white brush-stroke routes with red arrowheads that draw on over time. A route's thickness shows army size (it thins on the retreat). Small head icons ride the routes (Napoleon, Rasputin). Flame icons show scorched earth, skull-and-crossed-swords marks destroyed armies, and small flags mark foreign powers. Most map shots push in hard (zoom +50% to +230%).
- **Date tags:** a small white handwritten year or date ("1768", "December 1825", "February 1917") on a dark translucent box in the top-left corner. It appears a beat after the shot starts.
- **Text style:** one rounded handwritten-marker font throughout. Titles are white with a soft dark shadow. Small captions type on letter by letter (mid-word frames are common). Emphasis words are larger and coloured with a dark outline ("FIRST!", "ENOUGH!!", "RIOT!", "MILLIONS", "PEACE / LAND / BREAD"). Speech is plain white text placed near the speaker, with no bubble. Thought bubbles are white cloud shapes.
- **Real imagery:** oil paintings and archival B&W photos. Full-frame paintings get a slow Ken Burns push-in (+8% to +17%), with blurred, darkened copies filling the side margins when the aspect ratio doesn't fit. Portraits on the grey stage have rounded corners, a soft shadow and a white name caption at the bottom.
- **Infographic devices:** person-icon grids that turn grey to show losses, counters that roll up ("600,000", "23,000,000", "30,953 slices"), a hand-drawn line chart ("life of a serf → WORSE"), crossed-out items with a big red X, green check marks, a tear-off calendar, a signpost ("<- Home", "WAR"), and memorial candle rows on dark grey.
- **Transitions:** almost all cuts are hard. Explosions use a short cream/white flash frame (shots 285, 287). A dark overlay with a bright circle at the top-left appears on several date-change beats (247, 272/273, 278, 336), likely an iris-style wipe (inferred). Split-screen "letters vs reality" contrasts slide the room left and pop a new panel into the right half (240, 248).
- **Comedy beats:** gags include anachronistic jokes (a SUBSCRIBED button, an "ESCAPERS CLUB" banner, a "Corner Shop" Nicholas), deadpan two-person dialogue in corridors and cellars, and a heroic version of an event followed by a "small and messy" version. Punchline text is usually yellow.
- **Palette:** flat pastels for scenes: beige walls ~#E8DCC8, honey floor ~#C08A50, Kremlin red ~#C0392B, sky ~#C9DAEA, snow white. Accents are terracotta, mustard and navy. Night scenes use a navy sky ~#1E2238 with a white moon. Lighting is flat with no texture, apart from occasional vignettes in sombre scenes (cellar, memorial).
- **Shot rhythm:** shots usually run 2.5–8s, with 2–4 additive pop-ins per shot (often one per frame sample). The camera almost always does a slow push-in of +3–7% with a slight up-left pan. Elements pop in rather than slide, except for walking or charging characters and growing smoke or fire.

### Patterns noted in part 4
- **Narrator/host:** a stick-figure with round peach face, conical straw hat (tan, red tassel), red scarf/strap, holding a tall spear; sits at frame left (~10–13% x) on grey-background scenes or bottom-right (~87% x, cropped at waist) on maps. His reaction is the main beat: smiling at the shot start → "O" shocked mouth on the reveal → sometimes eyes-closed or grimace.
- **Plain grey "explainer" stage** (#c4c4c4): narrator left with content in the centre-right (photo cards, counters, personified countries, gauges, clipboards, calendars). It is used as the neutral palette-cleanser between rich scenes, roughly every 2–4 shots.
- **Photo cards:** real archival portraits (Gagarin, Gorbachev, Yeltsin, Putin) sit in rounded-corner frames with a soft drop shadow at about 60%,40%. They pop in with the name in white handwriting on the photo's bottom edge and a short dark caption written on beneath the frame (e.g. "former KGB officer").
- **Full-frame archival photos** (Stalin canal, tank, B-59, queue) get a slow Ken-Burns push (+6–13%) with blurred or dark pillarbox side bars and a white handwritten title written on at the top.
- **Maps:** satellite relief base (deep blue oceans, natural green/tan land), with the state of interest as a semi-transparent fill: USSR/Russia in rust-red (#9a3a2a–#d03a30), Germany grey, Ukraine olive-yellow or blue-grey, USA translucent blue. Labels are white handwriting; cities get yellow teardrop pins with yellow names; there is a small grey/white year tag at the top-left.
- **Map arrows** are thick white strokes with red arrowheads (invasions, routes), red translucent pincers for encirclements, red dashed circles for sieges and white dashed lines for partitions. Arrows grow/draw on across frames. Most map shots push in (+20–140%); a few zoom out to reveal distance (Trotsky to Mexico, Ukraine to Europe).
- **Text style:** rounded handwritten font that writes on letter by letter (partial words visible mid-shot). White is the main caption colour, yellow is for emphasis/subtitles, red/maroon for danger words ("SIEGE", "purged", "the Red Army", "Chaos!"), and small dark-grey for sub-captions. Second lines often add a twist ("from life" → "from history").
- **Big numbers** use large outlined white numerals with a small icon (person, skull) and a tiny label underneath. They count up across frames (2,998,673 → 3,000,000; 788,765 → 1,000,000; $509M → $1B).
- **Stamps/signs:** tilted red rubber-stamp rectangles ("THE DOCTORS' PLOT", "TOP SECRET") and boxed red words ("ORDER", "NO"), plus red X strike-throughs and red crossed-out text replaced by new red text ("Leave by 2024" → "Stay until 2036").
- **Character design:** flat cartoon people about 25–35% of frame height with thin stick legs and simple dot eyes. Soviets wear olive tunics; NKVD wear blue caps with red bands; peasants have red headscarves or black papakhas; navy officers wear navy uniforms (Arkhipov has a white cap). Leaders are recognisable by hair/moustache: Stalin (dark hair, moustache), Gorbachev (bald with birthmark), Yeltsin (white hair) and Putin (light thin hair).
- **Personified countries:** country silhouettes with eyes and stick limbs (USA blue, USSR red, Germany grey, Britain/France/Poland). They react with "!" marks and fall over to show collapse.
- **Scene sets are reused** with different characters: the countryside (cabin, barn, truck), the copper-pipe submarine interior, the Kremlin red wall with snowy ground, the retro brown TV set and the one-point-perspective rooms (pact room, office, throne room).
- **Transitions:** mostly hard cuts on narration beats. Occasional iris/circle wipes show up as a dark overlay with a circular edge at top-left, plus white flashes (pact photo flash, atomic bomb, explosions), brightness flicker for depth-charge shaking and cross-fades in the recap.
- **Typical shot length** is 2–7s, with almost always 2–4 discrete pop-in beats per shot (element appears → label writes → twist caption). The camera keeps drifting slowly (+2–7% push), so frames are rarely truly static.
- **Time-passing devices:** wall clocks with spinning hands, day calendars with a red header (DAY 7 → 13, NO PAY FEB, STILL GOING 2026), night→day sky change and a winter frost overlay on maps.
- **Recap device:** a top HUD timeline bar (dark rounded strip with five date medallions, the active one red) over quick scenes. The conclusion uses a cycle wheel (Chaos → One man → Order → Too far → Collapse) with a rotating pointer, and a parchment scroll timeline with terracotta medallions for chapter cards.
- **Palette:** light grey #c4c4c4 stage; Soviet red #c83a30; olive uniform #5a6a40; navy #1a2a4a; beige interiors #d8ccb0 with brown wood #6a4430; sky blue #c8d8e8 with white clouds; and dark dusk/night gradients with stars for dramatic moments.
- **Comic dialogue** is small white handwritten text with a dark outline placed near the speaker, without bubbles; round white speech bubbles are used only occasionally. Gag sequences include the photo eraser, the guards too scared to knock and the Gorbachev pizza advert.
- **Ending:** a teaser scene for the next topic (Ottomans), then a light-grey end screen reading "Next: The Ottomans" with two grey thumbnail placeholders and the narrator on the right.
